- Pinned by `TestResolveGeneratedTreeForUser_TwoUserCollision` and `TestResolveTreeIDForUser_ScopesToRequestingUser` (`internal/agentexec/wiring_test.go`), both of which save colliding same-ID trees for two users and assert each resolves their own.

---

## ADR-068: Distinguishing an Escalated-Origin HITL Resolution from a Routine Pending One (Closes the HITL-Escalation Program, Milestone 4/4)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability — stop HITL escalation from silently auto-approving workflow steps," milestone 4/4. ADR-061 (milestones 1–3) made `FindPendingByTaskID` match `StatusEscalated` alongside `StatusPending`, so `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID` could resolve an escalated request by task ID — but the callers of those methods gave no sign of which case had actually happened. `handleTaskApprove`/`handleTaskReject` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) returned the same `{"status": "approved"/"rejected", "id": ...}` shape regardless of whether the resolved request had been a routine pending approval or one a human operator had already escalated for review — and if `hitl.DefaultStore` held no matching request at all, the resulting error from `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID` was silently discarded, so the dashboard task still flipped to `"approved"`/`"rejected"` with no indication the HITL side had done nothing. The `bt_workflow_approve` MCP tool's by-task-ID branch (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) had the identical blind spot: it `json.Marshal`ed the resolved `*hitl.Request` verbatim with no signal of its originating status.

**Decision:** Both call sites now probe `hitl.DefaultStore.FindPendingByTaskID(taskID)` *before* calling `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID`, and derive `resolvedFrom := "pending"` unless the pre-resolution request's `Status == hitl.StatusEscalated`, in which case `resolvedFrom = "escalated"`. `handleTaskApprove`/`handleTaskReject` add `resp["hitl_resolved_from"] = resolvedFrom` on success, and — new — add `resp["hitl_note"] = err.Error()` on failure instead of swallowing the `hitl: no pending request for task %q` error from `hitl.DefaultStore`, so a task with no matching HITL request at all is now distinguishable in the response from one that resolved cleanly. `bt_workflow_approve`'s by-task-ID branch marshals an anonymous struct embedding `*hitl.Request` plus a `resolved_from` JSON field instead of the bare request, giving MCP callers the same signal REST callers now get.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 4/4; the "stop HITL escalation from silently auto-approving workflow steps" program is now fully landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A dashboard or MCP caller resolving a task by ID can now tell whether it was a routine pending approval or one that had already been escalated to a human, instead of the two cases being indistinguishable in the response.
- ✅ A task with no matching HITL request no longer silently reports `"approved"`/`"rejected"` on the dashboard REST path with zero indication the HITL side failed — the underlying `hitl.DefaultStore` error now surfaces as `hitl_note`.
- Pinned by `TestHandleTaskApproveReject_EscalatedVsPending` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), a table-driven test covering approve/reject × pending/escalated/no-request.

---

## ADR-069: `agent.RunDeps`/`RunOnce` Consult a User-Scoped Tree Resolver, Closing the ADR-010 Personalization Hardening Program (Milestone 5/5)

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal, and the explicit follow-up ADR-067 flagged in its own consequences: `agentexec.ResolveGeneratedTreeForUser`/`domains.ResolveTreeIDForUser` existed and were tested to resolve a slug-collided `goal:automate_<slug>` tree ID to the requesting user's own workspace instead of whichever colliding user's directory sorted first — but nothing on the real request path called them. `agent.RunDeps.ResolveTree` (`internal/agent/runner.go`) took only a tree ID, with no way to pass a requesting user through, and both production wiring sites — `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`'s `resolveTree` and `internal/agentexec/deps.go`'s `NewRunDeps()` — populated `RunDeps.ResolveTree` with the unscoped `domains.ResolveTreeID` alone. The collision ADR-067 fixed at the resolver level was therefore still fully live on the one path that matters: a scheduled personal automation's `RunOnce` call.

**Decision:** `internal/agent/runner.go` adds a `UserTreeResolver` type (`func(user, treeID string) *evolution.SerializableNode`) and a `RunDeps.ResolveTreeForUser` field of that type, alongside the existing unscoped `ResolveTree`. `RunOnce` resolves the agent's `Definition` first (as it already did), and if `def.Metadata["user"]` is non-empty *and* `ResolveTreeForUser` is set, it builds a closure over that owning user and uses it — instead of the bare `ResolveTree` — for both tree-ID lookups the function performs (the primary `treeID` resolve and the `agentName` fallback resolve). A nil `ResolveTreeForUser` or an unowned agent (`Metadata["user"] == ""`) falls back to the unscoped resolver unchanged, so existing unowned agents and any caller that never wires the new field keep current behavior exactly. `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` adds a `resolveTreeForUser(user, id string) *evolution.SerializableNode` wrapper over `domains.ResolveTreeIDForUser` and wires it into the daemon's `agent.RunDeps` literal; `internal/agentexec/deps.go`'s `NewRunDeps()` wires the equivalent closure for the Superpowers pipeline's in-process executor.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 5/5 of "Harden the ADR-010 personalization layer — user isolation, crash safety, and real task execution."

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A scheduled personal automation registered under a deterministic slug tree ID can no longer receive a different, alphabetically-earlier user's identically-slugged compiled tree — `RunOnce` now routes such a lookup through `ResolveTreeIDForUser` end to end on both production entry points (`bt-agent`'s daemon `RunDeps` and `agentexec`'s Superpowers-pipeline `RunDeps`).
- ✅ The scoping decision is made once, from data already in scope at `RunOnce` time (`Definition.Metadata["user"]`, set by `cmd/bt-agent`'s `activateAutomation`) — no new parameter threading through `RunAgent`/MCP tool signatures was needed.
- ✅ Unowned agents (no `Metadata["user"]`) and any `RunDeps` that leaves `ResolveTreeForUser` nil are unaffected — the unscoped `ResolveTree` path is exercised exactly as before.
- Pinned by `TestRunOnce_UsesUserScopedResolverWhenAgentHasOwner` (`internal/agent/runner_test.go`), which fails the test if the unscoped `ResolveTree` is consulted at all for an owned agent, and asserts the scoped resolver receives the correct user and tree ID.

---

## ADR-070: `NormalizeImpactSource` Exported from `internal/knowledge`, Closing Half of the CLI/MCP Normalization Split (Q1 Correctness Program, Milestone 1/3)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness — make `bt_impact_tests` honestly normalize and reject source paths like its CLI sibling," milestone 1/3. ADR-052 wired two consumers onto `knowledge.ImpactedTests`, but only the CLI subcommand got path normalization: `cmd/bt-agent-cli/impact.go`'s unexported `normalizeImpactSource` converted an absolute or cwd-relative path into the module-relative form the impact graph indexes and rejected anything outside `root`, while `cmd/bt-agent`'s `bt_impact_tests` MCP tool documented `source` as "already module-relative" and passed it straight to `knowledge.ImpactedTests` with no such conversion. Since `ImpactGraph.TestsFor` simply returns an empty slice for a key it doesn't recognize, an MCP caller that supplied an absolute path (or one relative to a different working directory) got a silent empty test list indistinguishable from "no tests are impacted," rather than either a correct lookup or an explicit error — and because the normalization logic lived only in the CLI package, `impact_tools.go` had no seam to reuse it.

**Decision:** Move `normalizeImpactSource`'s logic into `internal/knowledge/impact.go` as an exported `NormalizeImpactSource(root, source string) (string, error)`, alongside `ImpactedTests`, unchanged in behavior — abs-path resolution, module-relative conversion via `filepath.Rel`, and outside-root rejection. `cmd/bt-agent-cli/impact.go`'s `impactedTestsForSource` now calls `knowledge.NormalizeImpactSource` instead of a local copy, which is deleted. This milestone is a pure extract-and-export: `cmd/bt-agent/impact_tools.go`'s `impactTests` handler is untouched and still calls `knowledge.ImpactedTests` directly without normalizing `source` first, so the MCP tool's silent-empty-list gap for absolute-path input is not yet closed — that is milestones 2–3. Pinned by `TestNormalizeImpactSource_AbsoluteUnderRoot`/`TestNormalizeImpactSource_RejectsOutsideRoot` (`internal/knowledge/impact_test.go`, covering an absolute path under root, a plain relative path, and a path outside root), with the CLI package's own tests re-pointed at the shared function.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestone 1/3; milestones 2–3 (wiring `bt_impact_tests` to call `NormalizeImpactSource` and deciding how it should report an outside-root or malformed path) **resolved by ADR-071 (2026-07-13)**.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ The abs-path/module-relative/outside-root-rejection logic now has one home, `knowledge.NormalizeImpactSource`, instead of being reachable only from the CLI package — `cmd/bt-agent`'s MCP handler can now adopt it without duplicating the logic.
- ✅ `cmd/bt-agent-cli`'s observable behavior is unchanged — the same two cases (absolute-under-root, outside-root) are covered, now exercised through the shared package function instead of a local copy.
- ⚠️ `bt_impact_tests` still does not call `NormalizeImpactSource` — an MCP caller passing an absolute or differently-rooted path still gets a silent empty test list rather than a normalized lookup or an explicit rejection, the exact gap ADR-052 left open. **Closed by ADR-071 (2026-07-13):** `impactTests` now normalizes `source` before querying and returns `{"error": ...}` for a path outside `root`.
- Pinned by `TestNormalizeImpactSource_AbsoluteUnderRoot`/`TestNormalizeImpactSource_RejectsOutsideRoot` (`internal/knowledge/impact_test.go`).

---

## ADR-071: `bt_impact_tests` Normalizes and Honestly Rejects Out-of-Root Source Paths, Closing the Q1 Correctness Program (Milestone 3/3)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness — make `bt_impact_tests` honestly normalize and reject source paths like its CLI sibling," milestone 3/3, following ADR-070's milestone-1/3 extract-and-export of `knowledge.NormalizeImpactSource`. `cmd/bt-agent/impact_tools.go`'s `impactTests` still passed `source` straight to `knowledge.ImpactedTests` unnormalized: an MCP caller supplying an absolute path (or one relative to a different working directory than `root`) got a silent `{"tests": []}` — indistinguishable from "no tests are impacted" — instead of a correct lookup or an explicit error, while `cmd/bt-agent-cli`'s `impactedTestsForSource` (its sibling, already calling `NormalizeImpactSource` since ADR-070) honestly rejected the same input with an error.

**Decision:** `impactTests` now calls `knowledge.NormalizeImpactSource(root, source)` first and returns `{"error": ...}` immediately if it fails — the same shape `cmd/bt-agent-cli/impact.go`'s `impactedTestsForSource` already uses — before passing the normalized, module-relative result to `knowledge.ImpactedTests`. This is the same one-line composition as the CLI sibling, so the two callers of `NormalizeImpactSource` are now identical in shape as well as behavior. Pinned by `TestImpactTests_NormalizesAbsoluteSourceUnderRoot` (an absolute path under `root` now resolves to the same impacted-tests list a module-relative path would) and `TestImpactTests_RejectsSourceOutsideRoot` (a path outside `root` now returns `{"error": ...}` instead of `{"tests": []}`), both in `cmd/bt-agent/impact_tools_test.go`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestone 3/3; closes the Q1 Correctness program opened by ADR-070.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `bt_impact_tests` and the CLI's `impact` subcommand now share identical normalize-then-query behavior — an absolute or cwd-relative path resolves correctly under both, and a path outside `root` is rejected with an explicit error under both, closing the gap ADR-052 first documented and ADR-070 carried forward.
- ✅ No change to `bt_impact_tests`'s success-path response shape (`{"tests": [...], "source": ...}`); only previously-silent-empty-list failure cases now surface `{"error": ...}` instead.
- Pinned by `TestImpactTests_NormalizesAbsoluteSourceUnderRoot`/`TestImpactTests_RejectsSourceOutsideRoot` (`cmd/bt-agent/impact_tools_test.go`).

---

## ADR-072: Task-ID Collision Fix and a Priority-Ordered Approval Audit Trail for Dashboard Tasks (Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability Program, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability — wire the dashboard's tested Workflow/Approval audit trail into the actual thinktank→task→sprint execution path instead of leaving it dead code." `internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go` defines a fully tested `Workflow`/`WorkflowTask`/`Approval` model (approver/timestamp/reason audit fields, a `WorkflowPriority` ordinal, `sortTasks`/`Prioritize`) with zero production callers, while the live thinktank pipeline runs through a separate, simpler `dashboard.Task`/`TaskStore` (`internal/dashboard/tasks.go`) that `handleAnalyze` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) populates directly via `taskStore.Create`. Three concrete gaps existed on that live path: (1) `handleAnalyze`'s per-insight task ID — `fmt.Sprintf("tt-%d-%d", time.Now().UnixNano(), len(f.KeyInsights))` — keyed its second component on the loop-invariant `len(f.KeyInsights)` instead of the insight's own index, so two insights minted for the same fellow in the same nanosecond tick collided on an identical ID, and `TaskStore`'s `Get`/`UpdateStatus`/`SetOutput` linear-scan and act on the first match only, permanently orphaning the second task; (2) `handleTaskApprove`/`handleTaskReject` flipped only `Task.Status` to `"approved"`/`"rejected"` via `UpdateStatus`, discarding who decided and when, even though `workflow_engine.go`'s `Approval` struct already modeled that audit trail for its own (uncalled) `WorkflowTask`; (3) `TaskStore.Approved()` returned tasks in storage order, so `handleSprintExecute`'s dispatch loop ran whichever task was approved first rather than the highest-priority one.

**Decision:** Fix all three in place on the existing `dashboard.Task`/`TaskStore` types rather than migrating callers onto `workflow_engine.go`'s `Workflow` object (that migration remains undone — see Consequences). (1) `handleAnalyze`'s insight loop now captures its index (`for idx, insight := range f.KeyInsights[:min(2, len(f.KeyInsights))]`) and folds it into the task ID (`tt-<nanos>-<idx>`), so two insights from the same finding always get distinct IDs regardless of timestamp granularity. (2) `dashboard.Task` gains an `Approval` field of the same `Approval` type `workflow_engine.go` already declares; new `TaskStore.Approve(id, approver)`/`Reject(id, rejector, reason)` methods populate `ApprovedBy`/`ApprovedAt` or `RejectedAt`/`Reason`/`IsApproved` and persist through the existing save path, and `handleTaskApprove`/`handleTaskReject` call them instead of the bare `UpdateStatus` (the existing `hitl.DefaultStore` escalation bookkeeping on both handlers, ADR-068, is untouched). (3) A new unexported `priorityRank` maps `Task.Priority`'s string values onto `workflow_engine.go`'s `WorkflowPriority` ordinals (critical=0 … backlog=4), and `Approved()` sorts its result by that rank then by `Sprint` before returning — `handleSprintExecute`'s dispatch loop is otherwise unchanged and now runs in that order for free.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestones 1–3/3, closing this program's stated scope.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Two high-confidence insights minted for the same fellow in the same nanosecond tick no longer collide on task ID; each is independently retrievable and updatable. Pinned by `TestHandleAnalyze_TaskIDsKeyedOnInsightIndex` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`) — a source-level pin, since forcing a real nanosecond collision from a black-box test proved unreliable (0 collisions observed across 100k back-to-back `time.Now().UnixNano()` calls on the build host).
- ✅ Approve/reject decisions on dashboard tasks now carry an audit trail (approver identity, timestamp, and — for rejections — a reason) that survives a `TaskStore` Save/Load round trip rather than living only as an in-memory status string. Pinned by `TestTaskStore_ApproveRejectAuditPersistsAcrossSaveLoad` (`internal/dashboard/tasks_test.go`).
- ✅ `handleSprintExecute` dispatches approved tasks in priority order (critical → high → medium → low → backlog, then by sprint) instead of storage/creation order, mirroring `workflow_engine.go`'s own `WorkflowPriority`/`sortTasks` ordinal. Pinned by `TestTaskStore_ApprovedOrdersByPriorityThenSprint` (`internal/dashboard/tasks_test.go`).
- ⚠️ `internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go`'s `Workflow`/`WorkflowTask`/`RecommendationsToTasks`/`ApproveTask`/`ExecuteSprint`/`RunFullPipeline` still have no production caller: this program retrofitted the *shape* of its `Approval` struct and `WorkflowPriority` ordinal directly onto the already-live `dashboard.Task`/`TaskStore` types rather than routing `handleAnalyze`'s thinktank output through `Workflow.RecommendationsToTasks` or dispatching sprints via `Workflow.ExecuteSprint`. The pending→approved human gate `handleTaskApprove`/`handleTaskReject` already enforced (`Approved()` only ever returned tasks a human explicitly approved) is unchanged by this program; what changed is that the approve/reject decision itself is now audited and the approved queue is dispatched by urgency. **(Partially resolved 2026-07-13, ADR-080):** `handleAnalyze` now does route through `Workflow.RecommendationsToTasks`/`Prioritize`, making the engine's task-derivation half production-live; `ApproveTask`/`ExecuteSprint`/`RunFullPipeline` remain callerless. **(Further resolved 2026-07-13, ADR-081):** `PendingApprovals`/`ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` also gained production callers via new `/api/workflow/*` HTTP handlers, and `RunFullPipeline`'s auto-approval of `Priority <= PriorityHigh` tasks was removed — but that new endpoint trio operates on a `currentWorkflow` copy disconnected from the `taskStore`/`TaskStore.Approved()` path this ADR's dispatch-by-urgency fix actually governs, so the two approval mechanisms still do not share state; `ExecuteSprint`/`RunFullPipeline` remain callerless. **(Fully resolved 2026-07-15, ADR-116):** `RunFullPipeline` gained a production caller (`POST /api/workflow/run-full-pipeline`), closing this residual — `ExecuteSprint` is exercised transitively through it.

---

## ADR-073: `dashboard.PickTreeForTask` Routes Auction-Shaped Tasks to `auction_demo`, Exercising the A2A Auction on the Sprint-Execution Path

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research noted that `internal/a2a`'s announce→bid→award auction machinery (§8.12, ADR-008/064/065) was reachable only through an explicit `switch_tree` onto `auction_demo` behind its `IsAuctionTask` engine condition. The dashboard's own task-to-tree picker, `dashboard.PickTreeForTask` (`internal/dashboard/executor.go`), fed every task minted by `handleAnalyze`, manual creation, and the sprint-execution loop (§6.3) through its own independent keyword switch with no auction case, so an auction/delegation-shaped task created on that live path (e.g. from thinktank insight text mentioning "delegate" or "bid") could never actually route to the auction — it fell through to a generic tree pick instead.

**Decision:** Export `engine.auctionTaskKeywords` as `AuctionTaskKeywords` (`internal/engine/actions_a2a.go`) so a caller outside `internal/engine` can mirror `IsAuctionTask`'s routing signal without duplicating the literal keyword list. `PickTreeForTask` gains a new first-checked case, `isAuctionShapedTask(lower)`, matching any keyword as a whole word via a compiled `auctionKeywordPattern` regex (`\b(keyword1|keyword2|...)\b`) rather than a plain substring check, so an incidental mention (e.g. "unrelated to auctions", plural) doesn't falsely trigger routing the way `IsAuctionTask`'s own `strings.Contains` would. A match returns `"auction_demo"` ahead of the existing bug/build/security/research/test/refactor cases.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ The dashboard's thinktank→task→sprint execution path (§6.3) can now exercise the live A2A announce→bid→award auction on a real dashboard task, not only via an operator-issued `switch_tree` — closing a gap between the two production task-routing switches (`IsAuctionTask` in the engine, `PickTreeForTask` in the dashboard) that had silently diverged.
- ✅ Whole-word matching avoids a substring false positive `IsAuctionTask` itself doesn't guard against (e.g. a task merely mentioning "auctions" in passing); this is a stricter match than the engine condition, not a mirror of its exact semantics.
- ⚠️ The two routing decisions remain two independent implementations of the same keyword list (`engine.AuctionTaskKeywords`, consulted via `strings.Contains` in `IsAuctionTask` and via the whole-word `auctionKeywordPattern` in `PickTreeForTask`) rather than one shared predicate — a future keyword-set change must be re-verified against both call sites.
- Pinned by `internal/dashboard/executor_test.go` (auction/delegation-shaped task titles route to `auction_demo`; a passing mention like "unrelated to auctions" does not) and the existing `internal/engine/actions_a2a_test.go` coverage of `IsAuctionTask`.

---

## ADR-074: Bounded HTTP Timeout, Circuit Breaker, Retry, and Lock Scoping Harden the Knowledge-Graph Embedding Client Against an Unresponsive Ollama Backend

**Context (2026-07-13):** `EmbeddingClient.GetEmbedding` (`internal/knowledge/embeddings.go`) called `http.Post` directly, which uses `http.DefaultClient` — a client with no timeout configured. A Ollama backend that accepted the TCP connection but never wrote a response (hung, deadlocked, overloaded) left `GetEmbedding` blocked forever: both `BuildIndex`'s per-tree fan-out goroutines and the synchronous `discoverWithEmbeddings` discovery call had no bound on how long a single dead request could stall them. Repeated failures also re-dialed and re-timed-out the same dead endpoint on every single call, with no memory of prior failures. Separately, `Discover()` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) held `kg.mu.RLock()` for its entire body, including the call into `discoverWithEmbeddings` — so the same network round-trip that milestones 1–2 bounded to 2s still held the graph's read lock for that whole window, and a burst of concurrent `Discover` calls against a slow backend could starve `Register`/`Connect`/`RegisterEvolved`, which need the write lock. A transient failure (e.g. Ollama restarting mid-request) also had no retry: the very first dropped connection failed the caller and counted toward the circuit breaker, even though the backend might already be healthy again a moment later.

**Decision:** Milestones 1–2 (accepted earlier the same day) added two package-level guards wrapping every Ollama embedding call: `embeddingHTTPClient := &http.Client{Timeout: 2 * time.Second}` bounding every request, and `embeddingBreaker := reliability.NewCircuitBreaker("ollama-embeddings", 3, 60*time.Second)` gating `GetEmbedding` — mirroring the existing 3-state `reliability.CircuitBreaker` pattern (ADR-007) and the per-client breaker `ACPClient` already carries (ADR-054). Milestones 3–4 build on that foundation:
  - **Lock scoping (m3):** `Discover()` now takes `kg.mu.RLock()` only long enough to read `kg.hasEmbeddings()`, then releases it before calling `discoverWithEmbeddings`. `discoverWithEmbeddings` itself takes its own `kg.mu.RLock()` only around the in-memory `kg.Trees` similarity scan, *after* `GetEmbedding`'s network round-trip returns — so the lock is never held across the HTTP call. The keyword-matching fallback, `stringMatch`, takes its own `kg.mu.RLock()` for the same reason (it was previously covered only by the caller's now-removed `defer kg.mu.RUnlock()`).
  - **Jittered retry (m4):** the HTTP call inside `GetEmbedding` is now wrapped in `reliability.DefaultRetryPolicy()` — the same full-jitter (`FullJitterStrategy`) backoff policy documented in §8.5/ADR-007 — via `policy.ExecuteContext(ctx, ...)`, where `ctx` is bounded by `embeddingHTTPClient.Timeout`. A transient failure (dropped connection, Ollama mid-restart) gets one or more jittered retries within that shared deadline instead of failing immediately; a fully unresponsive backend still exhausts the 2s budget on the first attempt and the retry loop's own deadline check aborts without blocking through additional multi-second attempts. `embeddingBreaker.RecordFailure()`/`RecordSuccess()` now gate on the outcome of the whole retry sequence, not the first attempt.

Both `BuildIndex` and `discoverWithEmbeddings` remain callers of `GetEmbedding` with an unchanged signature, so the retry and lock-scoping changes are transparent to them: an exhausted retry still surfaces as the same `error` return `discoverWithEmbeddings` already treats as a clean fallback (empty id, zero score).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13). Milestones 1–4/5 of the "Q3 Reliability — harden the knowledge-graph embedding client against an unresponsive Ollama backend" program; milestone 5 is not yet implemented.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ An unresponsive (connection-accepting, non-responding) Ollama backend now fails `GetEmbedding` within the 2s timeout instead of hanging the caller indefinitely — pinned by `TestGetEmbedding_TimesOutOnUnresponsiveBackend`, which uses an `httptest.Server` handler that blocks on an unclosed channel and asserts the call returns within a 3s test deadline.
- ✅ After 3 consecutive failures the breaker opens and short-circuits further calls instead of hitting a dead backend on every request — pinned by `TestGetEmbedding_CircuitBreakerOpensAfterConsecutiveFailures`, which asserts the backend receives fewer HTTP requests than calls made once the breaker trips.
- ✅ `discoverWithEmbeddings` degrades cleanly (empty id, zero score) while the breaker is open, exercising its pre-existing error-path contract rather than a new one — pinned by `TestDiscoverWithEmbeddings_FallsBackWhenBreakerOpen`.
- ✅ A slow/hung embedding call in flight from `Discover()` no longer blocks a concurrent `Register()` — pinned by `TestDiscover_RegisterNotStarvedBySlowEmbeddingCall`, which races a `Register` call against a `Discover` call blocked on an `httptest.Server` handler that never responds, and asserts `Register` completes well before the embedding call's own timeout.
- ✅ A connection-level failure that resolves on retry no longer fails the caller — pinned by `TestGetEmbedding_RetriesOnceOnTransientFailureThenSucceeds`, which hijacks and drops the connection on the first request, serves a valid embedding on the second, and asserts `GetEmbedding` returns success after exactly one retry.
- ⚠️ `embeddingHTTPClient` and `embeddingBreaker` are unconditional package-level state shared by every `EmbeddingClient` instance (mirroring the package's existing `defaultEmbeddingClient` singleton) — a caller or test that constructs its own `EmbeddingClient` against a different `BaseURL` still trips and is gated by the one shared breaker. Tests requiring an isolated breaker swap it for the test's duration and restore it via `t.Cleanup` (`withFreshEmbeddingBreaker`, `embeddings_test.go`).
- ⚠️ Retrying inside the same `embeddingHTTPClient.Timeout`-bounded context means the jittered backoff delay eats into the 2s budget available for the retried attempt itself — a transient failure that occurs late in the window may not leave enough time left for a retry to complete before the shared deadline expires, in which case the call still fails (correctly) rather than blocking past the 2s bound.

---

## ADR-075: The Scheduler and DLQ Replay Dispatch Through the AgentRouter Instead of Calling RunOnce Directly, Closing Milestone 5/5 of the Horizontal-Scaling Substrate

**Context (2026-07-13):** ADR-011/ADR-012 built the `RemoteExecutor` + `AgentRouter` substrate and wired milestones 1–4 (construction from the live A2A card registry, dashboard `/api/scalability`, `bt-scalability-probe`), but left milestone 5 open: `agentRouter` was constructed in `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`, logged, and then never referenced again. Both live dispatch paths — the scheduler's `AgentRunner` closure passed to `globalSched.Start` and the DLQ replay executor installed via `dlq.SetReplayExecutor` — called `agentRunner.RunOnce(...)` directly. A peer joining the A2A card registry therefore had no way to ever receive a scheduled or replayed task; the router was reachable only from its own construction-site log line.

**Decision:** Thread `agentRouter` into both dispatch paths. The router's construction was moved earlier in `main()` — before the scheduler start and DLQ replay closures, which capture it by reference — building it first with only the local executor (`reliability.NewRouterFromEndpoints(localExec, nil)`) and folding in remote peers afterward via a new `AgentRouter.AddEndpoints(endpoints)` method once the A2A server's card cache is live, rather than reconstructing the router in place. Both closures now call `agentRouter.Execute(agentName, task)` instead of `agentRunner.RunOnce(...)`. Because the `AgentExecutor`/`AgentRouter` contract returns the thinner `reliability.AgentResult` rather than `agent.RunResult`, a new `AgentResult.Outcome` field (populated by `newLocalAgentExecutor`'s `LocalExecutor` from the underlying `RunOnce` result) carries the raw outcome string — including scheduler sentinels like the rate-limit carryover (§6.4/ADR-016) — across that boundary, and a new `routedRunResult` adapter reconstructs an `agent.RunResult` from it (falling back to deriving `"success"`/`"failed"` from `Success` when a backend, e.g. an unupdated remote node, leaves `Outcome` empty), so the retry-policy and DLQ-recording logic downstream of both closures is unchanged.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 5/5 of the "Horizontal-Scaling Substrate" program (ADR-011/ADR-012).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A peer that joins the live A2A card registry can now actually receive scheduled and DLQ-replayed work, not just tasks dispatched some other way — the substrate is reachable end-to-end for the first time
- ✅ Single-node deployments are unaffected: with no remote peers, `agentRouter.Execute` resolves to the same `LocalExecutor` path `agentRunner.RunOnce` took directly
- ✅ Scheduler sentinel outcomes (e.g. `goap_fusion_rate_limited`) survive the `AgentExecutor` boundary via `AgentResult.Outcome`, so `recordSchedulerAttempt`'s deferred-vs-failed distinction (ADR-025) keeps working once routed
- ⚠️ The `AgentExecutor` contract carries no `context.Context`, so the DLQ replay path's previous 30-minute deadline (`context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 30*time.Minute)`) no longer bounds a routed replay — an inherent limit of the router seam rather than a regression in this call site, since the same interface constraint applies to every `AgentRouter` caller
- ⚠️ `TraceID`/`SpanID` do not round-trip through `reliability.AgentResult`, so DLQ trace-parent linking degrades to the caller's own context for router-dispatched runs — a tracing-correlation loss only
- Pinned by `TestSchedulerAndDLQReplayDispatchThroughAgentRouter` (`cmd/bt-agent/main_test.go`), which asserts both closures reference `agentRouter.Execute(` rather than `agentRunner.RunOnce(`

---

## ADR-076: `bt_evolve_island` Wires Its `IslandModel.Bank` Field, Making Cross-Domain Experience Transfer Live in Production

**Context (2026-07-13):** ADR-062 implemented `IslandModel.Migrate`'s cross-domain experience transfer — copying elite mutation experience into a migrating individual's target domain via `Bank.SeedDomain` — behind an optional, caller-wired `Bank *ExperienceBank` field. The transfer was fully implemented and covered by `internal/evolution/island_model_test.go`, but `bt_evolve_island` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`), the sole production caller of `IslandModel.Migrate`, constructed its model via the bare `evolution.NewIslandModel(interval, rate)` constructor and never assigned `.Bank` — so the feedback loop ADR-062 closed was, in production, still dead: `Bank` stayed `nil` and `Migrate` never called `SeedDomain` no matter how many domains migrated.

**Decision:** `bt_evolve_island`'s handler now sets `im.Bank = deps.expBank` immediately after `im := evolution.NewIslandModel(...)`, wiring the handler's existing `ExperienceBank` dependency (already used elsewhere for mutation-experience recording) into the island model it constructs.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes the residual production-wiring gap left open by ADR-062.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Migration between domain islands in production now genuinely transfers experience: an elite migrating from a fitter domain into a weaker one seeds the target domain's `ExperienceBank` pool with up to 5 re-tagged entries from the source, exactly as ADR-062 specified
- ✅ No behavior changes for callers that never populate `expBank`: `Migrate` already treated a `nil` `Bank` as a no-op, so the assignment is safe regardless of deployment configuration
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveIslandWiresExperienceBankIntoMigration` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), which seeds a bank entry for one domain, runs `bt_evolve_island` with `migration_interval=1` across two domains, and asserts a re-tagged entry for the other domain appears in the bank after migration

## ADR-077: `pending_approval` Survives `engine.RunTask` as a Non-Terminal Outcome and Routes Through `TaskStateBridge` in the A2A Responder

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal, Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability. `internal/engine/hitl_gate.go`'s `HumanApprovalGate` already set `bb.Outcome = "pending_approval"` and returned `0` (Running) to signal a synchronous call is waiting on an external human, but nothing downstream honored that signal as non-terminal. `RunTask`'s multi-tick loop (`internal/engine/tree.go`) kept re-ticking on any `code == 0` return with no exit for a HITL wait — burning up to the 1000-tick `maxTicks` safety limit against a human who cannot respond within a single synchronous call — and its terminal `switch` recognized only `code == 1`/`code == -1`, so every other case, including a pending approval, fell into the `default` branch and was stamped `evolution.Partial`, discarding the real outcome. Symmetrically, `internal/a2a/server.go`'s `BTAgentExecutor.Execute` only special-cased `bb.Outcome == "success"`; every other outcome — including the now-correctly-preserved `"pending_approval"` — fell into the generic failure branch and was reported as `a2a.TaskStateFailed`, even though A2A already has `a2a.TaskStateInputRequired` for exactly this "waiting on more input" case, and `TaskStateBridge.BTToA2A` (`internal/a2a/task_bridge.go`) already existed as the intended single mapping point but had no case for `"pending_approval"` and was not even consulted by `Execute`.

**Decision:** Make `pending_approval` a first-class non-terminal outcome end to end. `RunTask`'s tick loop condition gains `&& bb.Outcome != "pending_approval"`, so it stops ticking the instant a HITL gate parks the blackboard rather than spinning to `maxTicks`; the terminal `switch` gains a `case bb.Outcome == "pending_approval"` (rewritten as an equality-switch since the prior cases were `code`-keyed) that leaves the outcome untouched instead of falling through to the `Partial` default. `TaskStateBridge.BTToA2A` gains a `"pending_approval" → a2a.TaskStateInputRequired` case alongside its existing `"input-required"` one. `BTAgentExecutor.Execute` is rewired to switch on `bridge.BTToA2A(bb.Outcome)` instead of the binary `bb.Outcome == "success"` check: `TaskStateCompleted` takes the success path unchanged, the new `TaskStateInputRequired` case yields a status-update event carrying the tree's own message (or a synthesized "awaiting input" one if the tree left `Result` empty), and every other state falls through to the pre-existing failure-reporting `default` branch, now parameterized on the resolved `state` instead of a hardcoded `TaskStateFailed`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A behavior tree that parks on a `HumanApprovalGate` mid-execution no longer has its wait silently collapsed to `"partial"` by `RunTask`, and no longer busy-spins the synchronous call up to 1000 times waiting for a human who cannot respond inside it. Pinned by `TestRunTask_PreservesPendingApprovalOutcome` (`internal/engine/tree_test.go`), which asserts the outcome survives and the gating action ticks at most once.
- ✅ An A2A caller driving a gated tree through `BTAgentExecutor.Execute` now sees `TaskStateInputRequired` — the protocol-correct "waiting" state — instead of `TaskStateFailed`, matching how the dashboard's own HITL path (ADR-056/061/068) already treats an approval wait as distinct from a failure. Pinned by `TestExecute_PendingApprovalRoutesThroughBridge` (`internal/a2a/server_test.go`).
- ✅ `Execute`'s outcome handling is now centralized in `TaskStateBridge.BTToA2A` rather than a duplicated binary check — the existing `"running"`→`TaskStateWorking` and `"input-required"`→`TaskStateInputRequired` bridge cases, previously unreachable from `Execute`, now take effect there too.
- ⚠️ An A2A caller still has no way to resolve the pending approval through the A2A protocol itself — resolution remains only through the dashboard's `/api/hitl/<id>/approve|reject` REST path or the MCP `bt_workflow_approve` tool; an A2A-native approve/reject is not part of this change.

## ADR-078: `persistEvolvedWinner`/`RegisterEvolved` Only Overwrite a Stored Evolved Winner When the New Fitness Actually Beats It

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal, Q1 Correctness. ADR-042 wired `persistEvolvedWinner` to persist every genetic-family evolve tool's bred winner to `"<baseTreeID>-evolved"` and register it in the knowledge graph unconditionally — a run's winner always overwrote whatever `<base>-evolved` tree file and KG metadata already existed for that id, regardless of whether the new run's fitness was actually better. Because `bt_evolve_genetic`/`bt_evolve_bottlenecks`/`bt_evolve_selection_pressure` are ordinary genetic runs whose population/generation parameters a caller controls per call, a later, weaker run could silently clobber a stronger elite a prior run had already earned and persisted.

**Decision:** Gate the overwrite on a real fitness comparison, with the knowledge graph as the single source of truth for "did this improve." `KnowledgeGraph.RegisterEvolved` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) now returns a `bool`: it still unconditionally connects the `baseID → evolvedID` `"evolved_from"` edge (that relationship holds regardless of which winner is currently stored), but the `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` bookkeeping write-back now runs only when the entry is new or `fitness > meta.StructuralFitness`, returning `false` without touching that bookkeeping when an existing entry's stored fitness already meets or beats the new one. `persistEvolvedWinner` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) is reordered to call `RegisterEvolved` first and branch on its return: when `false`, it sets `result["persisted"] = false` and `result["skip_reason"] = "fitness does not improve on stored evolved winner"` and returns without ever calling `persistGeneratedTree` — so a non-improving winner never reaches the tree-store file write either.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A weaker later genetic-evolution run can no longer overwrite a stronger `<base>-evolved` tree file or regress its KG `StructuralFitness`/`NodeCount` — both the file and the bookkeeping now only move forward. Pinned by `TestPersistEvolvedWinner_SkipsOverwriteWhenFitnessDoesNotImprove` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), which persists a strong winner, then a weaker one, and asserts `persisted: false`, the stronger tree still loads from `treeStore`, and `StructuralFitness`/`NodeCount` are unchanged.
- ✅ (amends ADR-042) The `evolved_from` edge still connects on every call regardless of the fitness outcome, matching ADR-042's original "discoverable either way" semantics — only the bookkeeping fields and the file write are now conditional.
- ⚠️ (amends ADR-042's caveat on unconditional registration) The order is inverted rather than the caveat being fully closed: `RegisterEvolved`'s bookkeeping bump now happens *before* `persistGeneratedTree` is even attempted, so a subsequent validation failure (`engine.ValidateTreeFull`) or a `nil` `deps.treeStore` can still leave `NodeCount`/`StructuralFitness` already bumped to the new winner's values with no matching tree-store file backing them — the same class of KG/file divergence ADR-042 flagged, just triggered by the later step failing instead of the earlier one. **Resolved by ADR-083 (2026-07-13):** the fitness check is split into a non-mutating peek run first, and the bookkeeping commit now runs only after `persistGeneratedTree` reports success.
- ⚠️ The comparison is strict (`fitness > meta.StructuralFitness`), so a new winner with fitness exactly equal to the stored one is treated as non-improving and skipped — ties never overwrite.

## ADR-079: `cmd/bt-gardener` Wires the Learned Selector-Ordering Pass into Its Production Daemon and MCP Tool

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal, Q2 Evolvability. ADR-029 (milestones 4–5) built the apply path for learned Selector-child ordering — `Gardener.evolveTreeV2` calls `applyLearnedSelectorOrdering` before `SaveTree`, gated on `EvolveV2Config.SelectorOrdering` and seeded from `Config.SelectorStatsPath` — but flagged as an open caveat that no production binary ever set either: `cmd/bt-gardener/main.go` built its `EvolveV2Config` via the bare `gardener.DefaultEvolveV2Config()` (which leaves `SelectorOrdering` off and `SelectorStatsPath` empty by design, opt-in), both for the daemon's own scheduled cycle and for the `gardener_run_cycle` langchain tool, which additionally rebuilt its own fresh default config on every call rather than reusing whatever the daemon had. So the tested milestone-4/5 pass ran only inside `evolve_v2_test.go`, never unattended.

**Decision:** A new `wireSelectorOrdering(cfg gardener.Config, metricsDir string) (gardener.Config, gardener.EvolveV2Config)` helper (`cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`) points `cfg.SelectorStatsPath` at `<metricsDir>/selector-stats.json` and returns an `EvolveV2Config` with `SelectorOrdering: true`. `main()` calls it immediately after building `cfg`, and `GardenerRunCycleTool` gains a `v2Cfg` field set once at construction (via the new `newGardenerRunCycleTool(g, v2Cfg)`) instead of calling `gardener.DefaultEvolveV2Config()` fresh inside `Call`, so both the timer-driven daemon cycle and every MCP-triggered `gardener_run_cycle` call now share the same wired config. Alongside this, a pre-existing `Registry.loadAll` bug (`internal/gardener/gardener.go`) that would otherwise have silently defeated the new production test coverage is fixed: a persisted tree file `tree-<name>.json` not already shadowing a builtin was registering under the literal name `"tree-<name>"` instead of the bare `"<name>"` that `SaveTree`/`addBuiltin` otherwise use, so a tree evolved and saved under a short name would, after the *next* process restart, only be resolvable under the doubly-prefixed name — orphaning it from any caller, including the gardener's own re-evolution passes, still looking it up by its original short name. `treeName` is now derived via `strings.TrimSuffix(strings.TrimPrefix(name, "tree-"), ".json")`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes the gardener half of ADR-029's milestone 4–5 production-wiring caveat.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ (amends ADR-029) The learned-ordering pass now runs unattended: every scheduled `bt-gardener` daemon cycle and every MCP-triggered `gardener_run_cycle` call reorders each tree's Selector children from accumulated telemetry before persisting, closing the "automatic loop still operator-triggered" caveat for the gardener call sites specifically. `domains.SelectorStatsPath`/`SelectorStatsPathFn` (the separate build-time tree-resolver seam noted in the same caveat) remains unset in production — a tree resolved fresh at request time is still not reordered; only the gardener's own persisted copies are.
- ✅ A persisted tree saved under a bare name (e.g. `custom_tree`, written as `tree-custom_tree.json`) now reloads under that same bare name after a restart instead of `tree-custom_tree`, so lookups, re-evolution, and the newly wired selector-ordering pass all resolve it consistently across restarts. Pinned by the updated `TestRegistry_PersistedTreeLoading` (`internal/gardener/coverage_test.go`).
- ✅ Pinned end to end by `TestWireSelectorOrdering_EnablesLearnedOrderingPass` and `TestGardenerRunCycleTool_CallAppliesLearnedSelectorOrdering` (`cmd/bt-gardener/main_test.go`), the latter seeding real telemetry, running a full `gardener_run_cycle` tool call, and asserting the persisted tree's Selector children come back in the learned order.
- ⚠️ `selector-stats.json` under `metricsDir` is a new durable file with no explicit cap or rotation, alongside the existing gardener metrics/reference files in the same directory (§7.2.2).

---

## ADR-080: `handleAnalyze` Adopts the `Workflow` Task-Derivation Engine and Stops Swallowing ThinkTank Orchestrator Errors (Q1 Correctness Program, Milestones 1–3/4)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness — Adopt the dashboard's ThinkTank→Workflow task-derivation engine in production and stop swallowing analysis failures." Two gaps coexisted after ADR-072: (1) `internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go`'s `Workflow`/`WorkflowTask`/`RecommendationsToTasks`/`Prioritize` remained callerless in production — ADR-072 deliberately retrofitted the `Approval`/`WorkflowPriority` *shape* onto the simpler `dashboard.Task`/`TaskStore` instead of migrating `handleAnalyze` onto `Workflow`, flagging that migration as an open consequence. (2) `handleAnalyze` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) called only `orch.RunResearchRound()`, discarding its error (`_ = orch.RunResearchRound()`), and derived tasks straight from `tt.ResearchFindings` — it never ran `RunDebate`/`RunSynthesis`, so `tt.Synthesis` was always nil and `RecommendationsToTasks` (which no-ops on a nil `Synthesis`) could never have produced a task even if called. The MCP counterpart, `bt_thinktank_analyze` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`), had the same discard on `orch.RunFullAnalysis(params.Topic)`, so a failed LLM call returned a 200-shaped response with zero findings indistinguishable from a topic that genuinely produced none. Separately, `Workflow.RunFullPipeline` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go:356`) unconditionally read `w.Tasks[0].Title` after `RecommendationsToTasks`, which panics whenever that call no-ops to zero tasks (nil synthesis or an otherwise-empty recommendation set).

**Decision:** Three milestones. (1) `RunFullPipeline` now guards the `w.Tasks[0]` read behind `len(w.Tasks) > 0`, leaving `Company.SprintGoal` at its prior value when there is nothing to summarize; `workflow_engine_test.go` adds nil-synthesis and zero-recommendation cases. (2) `handleAnalyze` now runs the full three-phase pipeline it needs to populate `Synthesis` — `RunResearchRound` → `RunDebate` → `RunSynthesis`, checking each error and returning early with an `{"error": ...}` JSON body on the first failure — then builds a `dashboard.NewWorkflow(tt.Name, tt, companyState)`, calls `RecommendationsToTasks()` + `Prioritize()`, and maps each resulting `WorkflowTask` onto a `dashboard.Task` (`Priority`/`Assignee`/`Sprint`/`StoryPoints`/`Approval` taken from `WorkflowTask.Priority.String()`/`AssigneeRole`/`SprintTarget`/`EstimatedEffort`/`Approval`) before `taskStore.Create` and the existing `PickTreeForTask` routing (ADR-073) — this is `Workflow`'s first production caller. (3) `bt_thinktank_analyze` now checks `orch.RunFullAnalysis`'s error and returns an explicit `{"error": ...}` tool result instead of proceeding with a half-populated `ThinkTank`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestones 1–3/4. Milestone 4/4 of the stated program is not part of this change. **Milestone 4/4 closed (2026-07-13, ADR-081).**

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A nil-`Synthesis` or zero-recommendation `RunFullPipeline` call no longer panics on an out-of-bounds `w.Tasks[0]` read. Pinned by `TestRunFullPipeline_NilSynthesis_NoPanic` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`).
- ✅ (amends ADR-072's ⚠️ consequence) `workflow_engine.go`'s `RecommendationsToTasks`/`Prioritize` are no longer callerless in production — `handleAnalyze` is a real caller, and because it now also runs `RunDebate`/`RunSynthesis` first, `RecommendationsToTasks` actually has a populated `Synthesis` to convert instead of no-oping. Dashboard tasks derived from thinktank output now carry real `AssigneeRole`/`SprintTarget`/`Approval` state pulled from `WorkflowTask` rather than being minted directly from raw `ResearchFindings` insight text. `ApproveTask`, `ExecuteSprint`, and `RunFullPipeline` itself remain test-only (`RunFullPipeline` gains a production caller in ADR-116). Pinned by `TestHandleAnalyze_UsesWorkflowForTaskDerivation` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`).
- ✅ A failed research round, debate, or synthesis call now surfaces as an explicit `{"error": ...}` response from `handleAnalyze` instead of silently falling through to an empty success body. Pinned by `TestHandleAnalyze_SurfacesOrchestratorError` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`).
- ✅ `bt_thinktank_analyze` now returns an explicit `error` field when `RunFullAnalysis` fails, instead of a 200-shaped tool result reporting zero fellows/findings/scenarios as if the topic genuinely produced none. Pinned by `TestBTThinkTankAnalyzeSurfacesOrchestratorError` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).
- ⚠️ `dashboard.Task.ID` is now built as `wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID`, and `NewWorkflow`'s `wf.ID` is `fmt.Sprintf("wf-%d", time.Now().Unix())` — second, not nanosecond, granularity — while `WorkflowTask.ID` values are fixed per-category strings (`rec-001`, `agree-001`, …). Two `handleAnalyze` calls landing in the same wall-clock second reuse the same task IDs, reintroducing the collision class ADR-072 fixed on the old `tt-<nanos>-<idx>` scheme, this time one level up in the ID construction `handleAnalyze` itself now owns. **Resolved by ADR-082 (2026-07-13):** `WorkflowTask.ID` now carries a random per-call nonce, so `wt.ID` — and therefore the composed `dashboard.Task.ID` — is unique even when `wf.ID` collides at second granularity.
- ⚠️ `RunFullPipeline` still discards its own `ttOrch` phase errors internally (`_ = ttOrch.RunResearchRound()` etc.) — this program's error-surfacing fix applies to `handleAnalyze`/`bt_thinktank_analyze`'s direct orchestrator calls, not to `RunFullPipeline`'s, which remains uncalled in production regardless. **Resolved by ADR-116 (2026-07-15):** `RunFullPipeline` now checks all five phase errors and halts on the first failure, and gained its first production caller.

---

## ADR-081: Workflow-Level Approval Endpoints Wired Over HTTP and `RunFullPipeline`'s Auto-Approval Removed (Q1 Correctness Program, Milestone 4/4 — Closes the Program)

**Context (2026-07-13):** ADR-080 closed milestones 1–3 of "Q1 Correctness — Adopt the dashboard's ThinkTank→Workflow task-derivation engine in production and stop swallowing analysis failures," explicitly leaving milestone 4/4 open: `internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go`'s `Workflow.PendingApprovals`/`ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` were fully unit-tested but had zero callers outside `workflow_engine_test.go` — `handleAnalyze` built a `*dashboard.Workflow` purely as a local variable to derive `WorkflowTask`s, copied them into `dashboard.Task` values, and let the `Workflow` itself fall out of scope, so the approval gate its own methods enforced could never be exercised outside a test. Separately, `Workflow.RunFullPipeline` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go:346-351`) auto-approved every task with `Priority <= PriorityHigh` as `"system"` immediately after deriving it, before `ExecuteSprint` ran — a bypass of the same approval gate the program was trying to make reachable, silently self-approving the majority of realistic workloads (critical/high priority tasks are the common case) the moment `RunFullPipeline` ran.

**Decision:** Two changes. (1) `handleAnalyze` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) now assigns the `*dashboard.Workflow` it builds to a new package-level `currentWorkflow` var (mirroring the existing `taskStore`/`companyState` package vars) instead of letting it fall out of scope. Three new handlers, registered alongside the existing `/api/tasks/approve`/`/api/tasks/reject` routes — `handleWorkflowPending` (`GET /api/workflow/pending`), `handleWorkflowApprove` (`POST /api/workflow/approve?id=`), `handleWorkflowReject` (`POST /api/workflow/reject?id=&reason=`) — call `currentWorkflow.PendingApprovals()`/`ApproveTask(id, "dashboard")`/`RejectTask(id, "dashboard", reason)` directly, returning 404 with an `{"error": ...}` body when there is no active workflow or the task ID does not resolve. (2) `RunFullPipeline`'s auto-approval loop is deleted outright; the method now goes straight from `Prioritize()` into `ExecuteSprint`, so every `WorkflowTask` — regardless of priority — stays `StatusPending` until an explicit `ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` call, and `ExecuteSprint` (which only ever advances `StatusApproved` tasks) advances nothing on its own.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 4/4; the "Q1 Correctness — Adopt the dashboard's ThinkTank→Workflow task-derivation engine" program is now fully landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `Workflow.PendingApprovals`/`ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` are no longer test-only — the three new HTTP handlers are real production callers, pinned by `TestHandleWorkflowApprovalEndpoints` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`).
- ✅ `RunFullPipeline` can no longer self-approve a task: `TestRunFullPipeline` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`) now asserts every task's `Approval.IsApproved` is `false` and its `Status` stays `StatusPending` after a full pipeline run with no explicit decision, and that `PendingApprovals()` still lists all of them.
- ⚠️ The new `/api/workflow/*` endpoints operate on `currentWorkflow.Tasks`, a separate in-memory copy of the tasks `handleAnalyze` also writes into `taskStore` as `dashboard.Task` values (ADR-080) under a different ID scheme (`wt.ID` vs. `wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID`). Approving or rejecting through `/api/workflow/approve`/`/api/workflow/reject` does not change the corresponding `taskStore` entry, and `handleSprintExecute`'s dispatch loop reads only `TaskStore.Approved()` (ADR-072) — so the workflow-level gate this milestone set out to make reachable is now reachable over HTTP, but not yet wired to the approval path that actually gates sprint dispatch. Closing that requires either dropping one of the two parallel `Task`/`WorkflowTask` representations or reconciling decisions made through either endpoint pair.
- ⚠️ `currentWorkflow` is a single unscoped package var overwritten by every `handleAnalyze` call. A second analysis run silently discards any pending approvals left over from a prior `Workflow`, and concurrent dashboard sessions share one workflow rather than one each. **Partially addressed by ADR-082 (2026-07-13):** the pointer read/write and every `Workflow` method's access to `Tasks` are now mutex-guarded, so concurrent HTTP handlers no longer data-race on this var or the slice it points at — but the single-workflow, last-write-wins overwrite semantics described above are unchanged; a second `handleAnalyze` call still discards a prior workflow's pending approvals, just safely rather than racily.
- ⚠️ `RunFullPipeline` itself remains uncalled in production (per ADR-080's residual consequence); this milestone's auto-approval removal is verified by its unit tests and takes effect the moment a production caller is wired, but has no live behavioral effect today. **Resolved by ADR-116 (2026-07-15):** now that `RunFullPipeline` has a production caller, this auto-approval removal takes live effect — every task it derives stays `StatusPending` until an explicit approval.

---

## ADR-082: `Workflow`-Derived Task IDs Made Collision-Resistant and `currentWorkflow`/`Workflow.Tasks` Made Concurrency-Safe

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal. ADR-080/081's residual caveats named two related gaps left by wiring `handleAnalyze` onto the `Workflow` task-derivation engine and exposing it over HTTP. First, `RecommendationsToTasks` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go`) minted `WorkflowTask.ID` as a fixed per-category string (`rec-001`, `agree-001`, …), and `NewWorkflow`'s `wf.ID` only carries second, not nanosecond, granularity (`fmt.Sprintf("wf-%d", time.Now().Unix())`); two `handleAnalyze` calls landing in the same wall-clock second produced identical composed `dashboard.Task.ID` (`wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID`) values, reintroducing the collision class ADR-072 had already fixed once on the dashboard's older ID scheme. Second, the package-level `currentWorkflow *dashboard.Workflow` var ADR-081 introduced was read and reassigned by `handleAnalyze`/`handleWorkflowPending`/`handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` from separate HTTP goroutines with no synchronization at all, and every `Workflow` method (`RecommendationsToTasks`, `ApproveTask`, `RejectTask`, `Prioritize`, `GetApprovedTasks`, `GetTasksByRole`, `GetTasksBySprint`, `PendingApprovals`, `ExecuteSprint`) read or mutated the shared `Tasks` slice with no lock — a genuine Go data race under concurrent requests, not just the already-documented last-write-wins overwrite semantics.

**Decision:** Two independent fixes in the same two files. (1) `RecommendationsToTasks` now mints one `newTaskNonce()` — 4 random bytes from `crypto/rand`, hex-encoded, falling back to `time.Now().UnixNano()` only if `rand.Read` errors — per call, and appends it to every `WorkflowTask.ID` it constructs (`rec-001-<nonce>`, `agree-%03d-<nonce>`, `disagree-%03d-<nonce>`, `dissent-%03d-<nonce>`), so IDs from separate `RecommendationsToTasks` calls can never collide regardless of `wf.ID`'s second-granularity timestamp. (2) `Workflow` gains an internal `mu sync.Mutex`; every exported method now takes `w.mu.Lock()`/`defer w.mu.Unlock()` before touching `Tasks`, and `GetTasksBySprint`'s body is extracted into an unexported `getTasksBySprintLocked` so `ExecuteSprint` — which already holds `w.mu` for its own duration — can reuse it without deadlocking on the non-reentrant mutex. In `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`, a new package-level `currentWorkflowMu sync.RWMutex` guards every read and write of the `currentWorkflow` pointer itself: `handleAnalyze` takes the write lock to reassign it, and `handleWorkflowPending`/`handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` take the read lock to snapshot the pointer into a local `wf` before calling any of its (separately, internally locked) methods.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ (amends ADR-080) Two `handleAnalyze` calls in the same wall-clock second no longer produce colliding `dashboard.Task.ID`/`WorkflowTask.ID` values. Pinned by `TestHandleAnalyze_TaskIDsUniqueAcrossAnalyses` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), which forces a second `Workflow`'s `wf.ID` to collide with the first and asserts the composed task IDs still don't, and the updated `TestRecommendationsToTasks_IDs` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`), which now checks per-category ID prefixes rather than exact IDs.
- ✅ (amends ADR-081) `currentWorkflow` and `Workflow.Tasks` are now guarded by `currentWorkflowMu`/`Workflow.mu` respectively. Pinned by `TestCurrentWorkflow_GuardedByMutex` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`) and `TestWorkflow_ApproveTask_SynchronizesOnMutex` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`), both of which hold the relevant mutex from an external goroutine and assert the guarded call blocks until it is released.
- ⚠️ Neither fix touches the last-write-wins semantics ADR-081 already flagged: a second `handleAnalyze` call still replaces `currentWorkflow` outright, discarding a prior workflow's pending approvals — now race-free, not multi-tenant.
- ⚠️ The nonce is per-`RecommendationsToTasks`-call, not per-`WorkflowTask` — every task minted by one call shares the same nonce, so uniqueness holds across calls but the four fixed category prefixes (`rec-001`, `agree-%03d`, …) remain the within-call disambiguator exactly as before.

## ADR-083: `persistEvolvedWinner`'s Knowledge-Graph Bookkeeping and Tree-Store Write Made Atomic

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal. ADR-078 gated `persistEvolvedWinner`'s overwrite of a stored `<base>-evolved` winner on a real fitness comparison, but its own consequences flagged that the fix inverted rather than closed the underlying KG/file divergence risk ADR-042 first noted: `RegisterEvolved`'s `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` bookkeeping bump ran *before* `persistGeneratedTree` was even attempted, so a subsequent `engine.ValidateTreeFull` failure or a `nil` `deps.treeStore` could still leave the knowledge graph claiming a fitness/node-count for `evolvedID` with no tree-store file backing it until the next successful run overwrote it.

**Decision:** Split the "does this improve" check from the "commit the bookkeeping" write, and order the write to only follow a confirmed successful persist. A new non-mutating `KnowledgeGraph.EvolvedFitnessImproves(evolvedID, fitness)` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) takes only `kg.mu.RLock()` and reports whether `fitness` would beat `evolvedID`'s currently-stored `StructuralFitness` (an unregistered `evolvedID` always "improves," matching `RegisterEvolved`'s own first-registration behavior). `persistEvolvedWinner` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) now calls this peek first and returns early (`persisted: false`, `skip_reason: "fitness does not improve on stored evolved winner"`) without ever attempting `persistGeneratedTree` when it is false — identical externally-observable early-exit behavior to before. When the peek passes, `persistGeneratedTree` runs as before; only if `result["persisted"]` comes back `true` does `persistEvolvedWinner` then call `RegisterEvolved` to actually commit the `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` bump and connect the `evolved_from` edge. `RegisterEvolved`'s own signature and internal fitness re-check are unchanged, so a concurrent writer that improved on the same `evolvedID` between the peek and the commit still can't be clobbered by a stale bookkeeping write.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes ADR-078's residual consequence.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ (amends ADR-078/ADR-042) A validation failure or `nil` `deps.treeStore` can no longer leave the knowledge graph's `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` bumped to a winner that has no backing tree-store file — the bookkeeping commit and the disk write now either both happen or neither does. Pinned by `TestPersistEvolvedWinner_AtomicWithFailedDiskWrite` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), which persists a strong winner, then a second winner with a higher fitness but an invalid node type, and asserts the KG entry still reflects the first winner's fitness/node-count/`EvolvedCount` rather than the failed second one's.
- ✅ The `evolved_from` edge's "connects regardless of fitness outcome" semantics (ADR-078) are preserved unchanged — only the `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` bump moved, not the edge connection, since `RegisterEvolved` itself is unchanged and still connects the edge on every call it makes.
- ⚠️ The two-step peek-then-commit is not a single atomic transaction across `deps.kg` and `deps.treeStore` — a fitness-improving winner from a concurrent call could in principle land its `RegisterEvolved` commit between this call's peek and its own `persistGeneratedTree` write, in which case this call's later `RegisterEvolved` still re-checks fitness internally and would correctly no-op rather than regress the winner, but the tree-store file itself would still be overwritten by this call's (non-improving, relative to the concurrent winner) write. No mutex spans both stores; this matches every other `persistGeneratedTree` caller's existing concurrency assumptions rather than introducing a new gap.

---

## ADR-084: `MarkSuccessful` and `EscalateToDeepSeek` Make the `OutcomeSelector`'s Self-Correction and Escalation Paths Real (Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability Program, Milestones 1–2/4)

**Context (2026-07-13):** The universal `PreGate→StrategyRouter→OutcomeSelector` pattern (§8.1) routes every task's outcome through a three-child `Selector`: `MarkSuccessful` → `SelfCorrect` (retried up to 3x) → `EscalateToDeepSeek`. `MarkSuccessful` (`internal/engine/registry.go`) unconditionally returned `1`, so the Selector's first child never failed — `SelfCorrect` and `EscalateToDeepSeek` were unreachable from any tree built on this pattern regardless of output quality. Compounding this, `EscalateToDeepSeek` was itself a no-op stub (`func(_ *btcore.BTContext[Blackboard]) int { return 1 }`) that made no LLM call and wrote no result, so even a tree that reached it would falsely report success.

**Decision:** `MarkSuccessful` now gates its `return 1` behind `validateOutputQuality(ctx.Blackboard)` — the same quality check already registered as the `ValidateOutput` condition (`internal/engine/conditions_core.go`) and used elsewhere for post-hoc scoring (§8.7) — returning `-1` when the blackboard's result is empty, too short, or otherwise fails the check, so the parent `Selector` actually falls through. `EscalateToDeepSeek` is rewritten to mirror the adjacent `SelfCorrect` action: it invokes `bb.LLM.Generate` with an escalation-framed prompt (task, plan-so-far, and previous output), writes the result to `bb.Result` and sets `bb.Outcome` to success only when the call succeeds, and returns `-1` on a `nil` LLM or a `Generate` error instead of always claiming success.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestones 1–2 of a 4-milestone program; milestones 3–4 (broader escalation-path coverage/telemetry) are not part of this change.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Garbage or truncated output (e.g., a `max_tokens`-starved chain) at the end of the primary strategy path no longer gets marked successful outright — it now retries via `SelfCorrect` and, if that also fails, actually escalates to an external LLM instead of silently succeeding. Pinned by new `internal/engine/registry_test.go` cases: `MarkSuccessful` returns `-1` for short/garbage blackboard output and `1` for valid output; `EscalateToDeepSeek` covers both the successful-escalation path (asserting `bb.Result` is written and `bb.Outcome` is success) and the no-LLM-available path (asserting `-1`).
- ✅ No tree topology changes — every tree already built with the `outcome()`/`NewDefaultOutcomeSelector` helper (`internal/domains/trees.go`, `internal/evolution/tree_builders.go`) gets the corrected routing automatically, since both actions are registered once in `registry.go`'s global `init()`.
- ⚠️ `EscalateToDeepSeek`'s name is now slightly misleading: like `SelfCorrect`, it calls whatever `bb.LLM` is configured (fallback chain included), not specifically a DeepSeek endpoint — this matches `SelfCorrect`'s existing behavior and the pre-existing naming convention, not a new gap introduced here.

---

## ADR-085: `RunTask`'s Terminal Backstop Flips `bb.Outcome` on Failed Quality Validation, Closing the OutcomeSelector Program (Milestone 4/4)

**Context (2026-07-14):** ADR-084 closed milestones 1–2 of "Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability — Make the OutcomeSelector's self-correction and escalation paths real" by gating `MarkSuccessful` on `validateOutputQuality` and giving `EscalateToDeepSeek` a real LLM call. But that gate — and the `OutcomeSelector` itself — only fires for trees built through the `outcome()`/`NewDefaultOutcomeSelector` helper. `RunTask` (`internal/engine/tree.go`) already called `validateOutputQuality(bb)` unconditionally at the end of every run as a stated defense-in-depth check, but discarded its boolean result — so a tree that terminates without ever routing through `outcome()` (e.g. a compiled GOAP fusion tree, whose terminal leaf just returns a bare success/failure code) could report `bb.Outcome = "success"` with garbage or truncated output and nothing downstream would ever catch it.

**Decision:** `RunTask` now acts on `validateOutputQuality`'s result instead of discarding it. After computing `qualityOK := validateOutputQuality(bb)`, if the run is not `bb.Sandbox`, `qualityOK` is false, `bb.Outcome` is currently `Success`, the resolved result (`resolvedResult`, a helper factored out of `validateOutputQuality` so both sites judge the same string) is non-empty, and the result is not one of `isStructuredOutput`'s recognized zero-LLM/structured markers (the same `"## "`/`route:`/`status:`/`delivered` heuristics `validateOutputQuality` already used, now extracted into a shared `isStructuredOutput` helper) — `bb.Outcome` flips to `Failure`. The `bb.Sandbox` exemption preserves gardener structural-evaluation runs, whose action stubs intentionally write short `"[sandbox] <name>"` placeholders with nothing genuine to judge; the non-empty-result exemption preserves a deliberate no-op success marker (`AlwaysSucceed`, used by guard/preflight composition) that produces no output to judge either.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14) — closes milestone 4/4 of the Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability OutcomeSelector program (ADR-084 closed milestones 1–2).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Trees that terminate without ever reaching `outcome()`'s `MarkSuccessful`→`SelfCorrect`→`EscalateToDeepSeek` chain — compiled GOAP fusion trees being the primary example — now get the same garbage/truncated-output defense the `OutcomeSelector` enforces elsewhere, closing the gap ADR-084 left open. Pinned by three new `internal/engine/coverage_test.go` cases: `TestRunTask_TerminalBackstopFlipsOutcome` (a bare Action writing `"bad"` flips to Failure), `TestRunTask_TerminalBackstopSkipsStructuredOutput` (a `"## X"` result stays Success), and `TestRunTask_TerminalBackstopSkipsSandboxMode` (a `Sandbox`-mode run stays Success regardless of output).
- ⚠️ Because the backstop is unconditional across every `RunTask` call (not scoped to GOAP fusion trees specifically), several pre-existing engine test fixtures whose canned mock/action output was shorter than `validateOutputQuality`'s 30-character minimum (e.g. `"action executed"`, `"code path selected"`, `"Hello! How can I help?"`) had never had their `bb.Outcome` checked before and needed lengthening to stay above threshold — `internal/engine/chains_test.go`, `decision_tree_test.go`, `edge_case_coverage_test.go`, and `internal/blackboard/engine_integration_test.go` all updated their fixture strings accordingly, with no behavioral change to the code under test.
- ⚠️ `resolvedResult`/`isStructuredOutput` are now shared between `validateOutputQuality` and the terminal backstop; a future change to either heuristic changes both call sites identically, which is the intended behavior but is worth remembering if one is ever meant to diverge from the other.

---

## ADR-086: `handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` Mirror Their Decision into `taskStore`, Closing the Approval-Surface Split ADR-081 Left Open

**Context (2026-07-14):** ADR-081 gave `Workflow.PendingApprovals`/`ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` their first production callers — `GET /api/workflow/pending`, `POST /api/workflow/approve`, `POST /api/workflow/reject` — but flagged in its own consequences that these endpoints mutate only the in-memory `currentWorkflow.Tasks` copy, keyed on the bare `WorkflowTask.ID` (e.g. `rec-001`). `handleAnalyze` separately persists each derived `WorkflowTask` into `taskStore` as a `dashboard.Task`, keyed on the composed ID `wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID` (ADR-080), and `handleSprintExecute`'s dispatch loop reads exclusively from `TaskStore.Approved()` (ADR-072). The two records never stayed in sync: approving a task through `/api/workflow/approve` left its `taskStore` counterpart stuck at `Status: "pending"` forever, so the approval was reachable over HTTP but had no effect on what a sprint actually dispatched.

**Decision:** `handleWorkflowApprove` and `handleWorkflowReject` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) now also call `taskStore.Approve(wf.ID+"-"+taskID, "dashboard")` / `taskStore.Reject(wf.ID+"-"+taskID, "dashboard", reason)` immediately after the corresponding `Workflow.ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` call succeeds, composing the same `wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID` ID `handleAnalyze` used when it created the record. Both calls' errors are discarded (`_ = ...`), matching the existing handlers' error-handling style for this best-effort mirror — the primary HTTP response still reflects the `Workflow`-side outcome.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14) — closes the residual gap ADR-081 flagged.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Approving or rejecting a task through `/api/workflow/approve`/`/api/workflow/reject` now updates the same `taskStore` record `handleSprintExecute` dispatches from — the workflow-level approval gate is not just reachable over HTTP (ADR-081) but actually governs sprint dispatch. Pinned by `TestHandleWorkflowApproveReject_UpdatesTaskStore` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), which seeds a `taskStore` record under the composed ID, approves/rejects through the workflow endpoints, and asserts both the `taskStore` record's `Status`/`Approval.IsApproved` and its presence in `taskStore.Approved()`.
- ⚠️ If `taskStore` has no record under the composed ID — e.g. a task approved through `/api/workflow/approve` that was never routed through `handleAnalyze`'s `taskStore.Create` call — `taskStore.Approve` returns an error that is silently discarded; the `Workflow`-side decision still succeeds and is reflected in `currentWorkflow.Tasks`, but nothing lands in `taskStore`. This matches `handleAnalyze`'s own always-paired construction (every `WorkflowTask` it derives gets both a `currentWorkflow.Tasks` entry and a `taskStore.Create` call), so the gap is theoretical for the one production code path that builds both records, not a live risk.
- ⚠️ The two records remain independently owned and separately mutable (`Workflow.Tasks[i]` directly, `dashboard.Task` via `TaskStore`'s own methods) — this fix makes the one round-trip `handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` need reliable, not a general reconciliation between the two representations. A task approved directly through `/api/tasks/approve` (ADR-072) still does not update `currentWorkflow.Tasks`, though that direction was never the one gating sprint dispatch.

---

## ADR-087: `handleAgentExecute` Carries `RunResult.Quality` into `AgentResult.QualityScore` for Remote-Routed Runs

**Context (2026-07-14):** `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`'s in-process `newLocalAgentExecutor` (the `LocalExecutor` adapter ADR-011 introduced for the horizontal-scaling substrate) has always set `QualityScore: res.Quality` straight from `agent.RunOnce`'s result. The dashboard's HTTP counterpart, `handleAgentExecute` (`POST /api/agents/execute` — the endpoint `reliability.RemoteExecutor.Execute` calls into, per ADR-011/ADR-075, whenever an `AgentRouter` routes a task to a remote dashboard node), built its `reliability.AgentResult` from `AgentExecutor.RunTask`'s `(output, outcome, err)` triple, which drops the `agent.RunResult.Quality` value the richer `RunTaskResult` method (which `RunTask` itself wraps) had already computed. Every remote-routed successful run therefore reported `QualityScore: 0.0` regardless of its actual quality — a silent blind spot for any fitness/quality tracking downstream of a routed, as opposed to purely local, execution.

**Decision:** `handleAgentExecute` now calls `executor.RunTaskResult(req.Agent, req.Task, treeID)` (already implemented in `internal/dashboard/executor.go`) instead of `RunTask`, and a new `agentExecuteResult` helper builds the `reliability.AgentResult` from the returned `*agent.RunResult` — copying `Output`, `Quality` into `QualityScore`, and deriving `Success` from `Outcome` exactly as the old inline code did — collapsing the two duplicated inline blocks (worker-pool path and synchronous-fallback path) into one shared function in the process.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Remote-routed agent runs dispatched through `/api/agents/execute` now report a real `QualityScore`, matching the in-process `LocalExecutor` path's behavior — closing a gap that would otherwise widen as more nodes join the `AgentRouter` registry (ADR-011/ADR-075). Pinned by `TestHandleAgentExecute_SetsQualityScoreFromRunResult` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), which forces the synchronous fallback path with a bare `AlwaysSucceed` tree and asserts `QualityScore > 0`.
- ✅ The worker-pool and synchronous-fallback branches of `handleAgentExecute` no longer duplicate the `AgentResult`-construction logic — both now call the same `agentExecuteResult` helper.
- ⚠️ `TraceID`/`SpanID` still do not round-trip through `reliability.AgentResult` (ADR-075's pre-existing caveat) — this fix only addresses `QualityScore`, not the tracing-correlation gap.

## ADR-088: `evolution.MetaValidator` Wired into the Gardener's Live Mutation-Acceptance Loop

**Context (2026-07-14):** `evolution.MetaValidator` (`internal/evolution/meta_validator.go`) combines structural validation (root type, node names, size/depth limits, selector shape, retry bounds), `ExpertKnowledge` anti-pattern checks, archetype fit, and fitness-floor/regression gates into one explainable `MetaAccept`/`MetaWarn`/`MetaReject` decision — a P0 safety layer distinct from and stricter than composite-score-only acceptance. It had existed fully implemented and unit-tested since its own introduction but had zero production callers: `Gardener.evolveTreeV2` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`) accepted a candidate mutation once `QuickValidate`'s benchmark score, the pre-score regression check, and `QualityGate.ValidateFor` all passed, so a candidate that scored well on those composite metrics alone — e.g. one that emptied out a Selector's children, or introduced an unbounded retry loop, or matched a known expert anti-pattern — could still be committed to the live tree.

**Decision:** `gardener.Config` gains an optional `MetaValidator *evolution.MetaValidator` field. `evolveTreeV2`'s per-candidate acceptance loop now calls `MetaValidator.ValidateMutation(tree, candidateTree, currentFitness.Composite, candidateFitness.Composite)` — `tree` as the pre-mutation baseline, the mutated clone as candidate — immediately after `QualityGate.ValidateFor` accepts a candidate and before `ApplyMutations` commits it to the live tree; a `MetaReject` decision rejects the candidate (counted the same as any other rejection) regardless of its fitness score.
