## ADR-126: `/api/trees` Surfaces Fitness and Evolution Lineage, Closing Milestones 1–2/4 of the Dashboard's Dead Blind Spot (Q1 Correctness / Q2 Evolvability)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q1 Correctness / Q2 Evolvability — Surface knowledge-graph fitness and evolution lineage from the dashboard's dead `/api/trees` blind spot." `RegisterEvolved` (ADR-042) has written `evolved_from` edges and maintained `StructuralFitness`/`EvolvedCount` since 2026-07-13, and `DiscoverRelated` has walked those edges since even earlier — but `handleTrees` (`GET /api/trees`, `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) returned only `id`/`name`/`category`/`node_count`, so none of that knowledge-graph state — fitness, structural fitness, run count, evolved count, last outcome, or lineage — ever reached the dashboard. The Evolution tab's "Algorithms Active" panel and `best_fitness` stat were sourced only from the disconnected `gardener-metrics.json` aggregate (§8.11 ADR-032), never from live `KnowledgeGraph` state.

**Decision:** **Milestone 1:** A new typed `KnowledgeGraph.EvolutionLineage(treeID string) (baseID string, evolvedIDs []string, ok bool)` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`, §8.5) resolves the `evolved_from`-edge lineage for a tree in either direction — base→descendants or descendant→base-plus-siblings — returning `ok=false` when none exists. **Milestone 2:** `handleTrees` adds `fitness`, `structural_fitness`, `run_count`, `evolved_count`, `last_outcome`, and (when `EvolutionLineage` reports one) a `lineage: {base_id, evolved_ids}` object to each `/api/trees` entry; `handleMetricsLive` separately builds a `[]dashboard.TreeSnapshot` per tree and passes it to a `Collect` now widened to a third parameter, which stamps a new `Metrics.TopWinners` field via `rankTopWinners`'s descending-`StructuralFitness` sort (§5.4, §8.11).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — milestones 1–2/4 landed; **milestone 3/4 (Evolution-tab UI) landed only its `metrics.go`/`TopWinners` backend half** — `cmd/bt-dashboard/static/js/tabs/evolution.js` was not modified, so the "Algorithms Active" panel and its `gardener.best_fitness` stat still render exactly as before ADR-032; nothing in the UI reads `TopWinners` or the new `/api/trees` fields yet. ~~Milestone 4/4 is not yet started.~~ — resolved 2026-07-16 (ADR-127): `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` now consults `EvolutionLineage` before re-evolving a tree with a fitter, non-regressing evolved descendant. Milestone 3/4's UI-wiring gap above is untouched by that fix and remains separately open.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `evolved_from` edges and `StructuralFitness`/`EvolvedCount` bookkeeping that `RegisterEvolved` has maintained since ADR-042 finally have a query API (`EvolutionLineage`) and an HTTP consumer (`/api/trees`) — the data was always correct, just unreachable outside `DiscoverRelated`'s fuzzy discovery match and direct KG inspection.
- ✅ `/api/trees` responses are additive and backward-compatible: existing `id`/`name`/`category`/`node_count` fields are unchanged, `lineage` is omitted (not null) for a tree with no evolution history.
- ⚠️ The Evolution tab is not yet live: `Metrics.TopWinners` and the enriched `/api/trees` payload have no browser-side consumer, so an operator looking at the dashboard today still sees the same static panel and `gardener-metrics.json`-sourced stat this program set out to replace. The backend/frontend split means this ADR should not be read as "the Evolution tab now reflects KnowledgeGraph state" — only its data source does.
- Pinned by `TestEvolutionLineage_BaseToEvolved`, `TestEvolutionLineage_EvolvedToBase`, `TestEvolutionLineage_NotFound` (`internal/knowledge/graph_discover_test.go`, milestone 1), `TestHandleTrees_IncludesFitnessAndLineage` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`, milestone 2), and `TestCollect_SurfacesTopEvolvedWinnersFromTrees` (`internal/dashboard/metrics_test.go`, milestone 2).

---

## ADR-127: `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` Consults `EvolutionLineage` Before Re-Evolving a Tree With a Fitter, Non-Regressing Evolved Descendant, Closing Milestone 4/4 of ADR-126's Program (Q1 Correctness / Q2 Evolvability)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Milestone 4/4 of the "Q1 Correctness / Q2 Evolvability — Surface knowledge-graph fitness and evolution lineage from the dashboard's dead `/api/trees` blind spot" program ADR-126 began. `RegisterEvolved`'s own doc comment (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) has described a benefit of the `evolved_from` edge it writes since ADR-042 — so fitness-aware discovery and the gardener can find the bred winner on the next run — that no code path actually acted on: `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' per-bottleneck loop (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) unconditionally spent a fresh CMA-ES or genetic-evolution budget on every detected bottleneck, even one whose earlier run had already produced and registered a fitter evolved descendant via that exact bookkeeping.

**Decision:** `KnowledgeGraph.EvolutionLineageBestFitness(treeID string) (fitness float64, ok bool)` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) resolves `treeID`'s lineage through the existing `EvolutionLineage` (ADR-126) and returns the highest `StructuralFitness` among `treeID`'s own evolved descendants; `ok=false` when `treeID` has none of its own (no lineage at all, or `treeID` is itself the evolved side of some other base — querying a descendant's siblings is deliberately out of scope). A new `lineageSkipsReEvolution(kg, treeID, baseFitness)` helper (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) combines that with the tree's shared `TrackRecord` archive at `trackRecordArchivePath(treeID)` — the same per-tree archive `bt_evolve_qd`/`bt_evolve_multiobjective`/`bt_evolve_pareto`/`bt_evolve_qlearning`/`bt_evolve_island` already record benchmark-gate accept/reject outcomes to (ADR-119): it reports true only when a descendant's fitness beats `baseFitness` (the bottleneck's runtime `SuccessRate`) *and* `track.WinRate() >= 0.5` — a recent history of more benchmark-gate rejections than acceptances means the stored evolved winner may no longer be trustworthy, so re-evolution proceeds instead of deferring to a possibly-stale lineage. `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' per-bottleneck loop calls this immediately after the existing baseTree-resolution check and before either the CMA-ES or genetic branch runs; a skipped tree is appended to a new `lineage_skipped` result list (mirroring the pre-existing `skipped` list for unresolvable trees) instead of silently dropping out of `report`, and contributes no entry to the `algorithms` tally.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — milestone 4/4, closing the program ADR-126 began.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Closes the loop `RegisterEvolved`'s doc comment has described since ADR-042: a bottleneck tree with an already-fitter, non-regressing evolved descendant no longer burns another evolution budget on itself every time `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` runs.
- ✅ The gate is `TrackRecord`-aware, not fitness-only: a tree whose stored evolved winner has recently been failing `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner` re-evolves anyway rather than deferring forever to a fitness number live benchmark runs no longer back.
- ✅ Skipped bottlenecks stay observable via the new `lineage_skipped` list, distinct from the pre-existing `skipped` (unresolvable-tree) list.
- ⚠️ `EvolutionLineageBestFitness` only resolves `treeID` as a lineage *base*; a bottleneck that is itself an evolved descendant of some other tree is not covered by this check, matching `EvolutionLineage`'s own base→descendants query shape.
- ⚠️ The `TrackRecord` archive this reads is written by the five benchmark-gated evolve tools (ADR-119), not by `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` itself — its `WinRate()` gate here reflects those other tools' history on the same tree, not any history of its own.
- ⚠️ This closes milestone 4/4 as scoped (a re-evolution-gating consumer of `EvolutionLineage`); it does not touch milestone 3/4's still-open gap (ADR-126): the Evolution-tab UI still has no browser-side consumer for `TopWinners` or `/api/trees`'s new fields.
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveBottlenecksSkipsTreeWithFitterNonRegressingEvolvedDescendant` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

---

## ADR-128: `ACPClient.GenerateCtx` Guards Its `cmd.Stderr` Buffer With a Mutex, Closing a Data Race Between the Subprocess's Copy Goroutine and the Timeout/Scan-Error Read Path (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-16):** NotebookLM research goal 1: fix the `internal/llm/acp.go` stderr data race in `ACPClient.GenerateCtx`. `GenerateCtx` set `cmd.Stderr` to a plain `*bytes.Buffer`. Because that target is not an `*os.File`, Go's `os/exec` package starts its own goroutine that continuously copies the subprocess's stderr pipe into the buffer for as long as the subprocess is alive — a detail internal to `exec.Cmd`, not visible from `GenerateCtx`'s own code. Meanwhile `GenerateCtx`'s `request` closure reads `stderr.String()` on its `ctx.Done()`/`scanErr` branches to enrich a timeout or failure error, with no synchronization against that background copy goroutine — a live `bytes.Buffer.Write`/`bytes.Buffer.String` data race whenever the context deadline or a scan error fires while the subprocess is still writing to stderr, exactly the case of a hung or crash-looping ACP subprocess the surrounding call path already hardens against (ADR-053/ADR-054).

**Decision:** A new `syncBuffer` type (`internal/llm/acp.go`) wraps a `bytes.Buffer` with a `sync.Mutex`, exposing `Write` and `String` methods that both take the lock; `cmd.Stderr = &stderr` now holds a `*syncBuffer` in place of the bare `*bytes.Buffer`. No other logic in `GenerateCtx` changes — the same `ctx.Done()`/`scanErr` branches call `stderr.String()` exactly as before, now synchronized against `os/exec`'s copy goroutine instead of racing it.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `GenerateCtx`'s stderr-enriched timeout/scan-error path is race-free under `-race` even when the subprocess is still alive and actively writing to stderr at the moment the deadline fires.
- ✅ No behavior change for callers: `stderr.String()` still returns whatever content has been captured so far, only now safely.
- Pinned by `TestACPClientGenerateCtx_StderrBufferRace` (`internal/llm/acp_test.go`), which drives a helper subprocess that floods stderr continuously and never responds on stdout, forcing the context deadline to fire while the copy goroutine is still writing.

## ADR-129: `setGoapStateDurable` Makes GOAP Fusion Charge Stamps Durable, and `clearSuperpowersPlanState` Retires Them on Cycle Completion (Q3 Reliability, Milestones 1–2/2)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q3 Reliability — Persist GOAP fusion research-goal/milestone charge stamps durably so resumed cron ticks charge and refund correctly." `PrioritizeGoapGoals` (`internal/engine/actions_goap_fusion.go`) stamps four keys every cycle it queues a program milestone or a research goal — `program_milestone_charged`, `program_milestone`, `research_goal_charged`, `research_goal_charged_text` — via the plain `setGoapState`, which writes ChainState only. Per §6.4/ADR-016's already-established durable pattern, ChainState dies with the run whenever a cron tick builds a fresh `Blackboard` (`RunOnce`), so these stamps had no memory across such a boundary, unlike the saved-plan path (`loadSuperpowersPlanState`/`saveSuperpowersPlanState`) and the Claude backoff deadline, both of which already fall back to the agent-scope blackboard. Separately, `clearSuperpowersPlanState` — called from every site that retires a completed or abandoned Superpowers cycle (`actions_superpowers_prod.go:804,917,1066`) — wiped only the two plan-resume keys, never the four charge stamps, so a stamp from a just-finished cycle could survive into a later, unrelated cycle and have `chargeGoapResearchGoalFailure`/`refundGoapMilestoneAttemptForInfraFailure` charge or refund the wrong goal or milestone.

**Decision:** **Milestone 1:** a new `setGoapStateDurable(bb, key, value)` calls `setGoapState` and then, when `bb.BB`/`bb.BB.AgentName` are set, also writes `"goap_fusion_"+key` into the agent-scope blackboard (`blackboard.Scope{Kind: ScopeAgent, ID: AgentName}`) — the same shape `saveSuperpowersPlanState` already uses. `PrioritizeGoapGoals`'s four stamp sites (`actions_goap_fusion.go:341,361,381,385`) now call it instead of `setGoapState`. **Milestone 2:** `clearSuperpowersPlanState` (`actions_goap_fusion.go:781`) now iterates a six-key list — `superpowers_plan_path`, `superpowers_active_plan`, `program_milestone_charged`, `program_milestone`, `research_goal_charged`, `research_goal_charged_text` — deleting each from both ChainState and the agent-scope store, in place of the two hardcoded deletes it had.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ The four charge stamps now survive a ChainState-only death: any reader that consults the agent-scope blackboard directly (not yet any production reader — see below) can recover which milestone or research goal a cycle charged even after the originating `Blackboard` is gone.
- ✅ `clearSuperpowersPlanState` can no longer leak a completed/abandoned cycle's charge stamp into a later cycle's failure handler; all three of its call sites get this for free with no change to their own code.
- ⚠️ The failure/refund readers themselves — `chargeGoapResearchGoalFailure` (`actions_goap_fusion_goal_budget.go:60`) and the refund path's `goapChargedMilestoneRef` (`actions_goap_fusion_refund.go:122`) — still read the stamp from `bb.ChainState` only, with no agent-scope fallback. A tick that resumes a saved plan straight into `Implement` (§6.4's Preflight "resume saved plan" branch) never re-runs `PrioritizeGoapGoals`, so its fresh, empty ChainState still has nothing for these readers to find on a genuine failure — the stamp is durable on disk, but not yet read back. The program's own title ("so resumed cron ticks charge and refund correctly") is therefore not yet fully realized; a follow-up must add the agent-scope read-back to close it.
- Pinned by `TestPrioritizeGoapGoals_StampsResearchGoalDurably` (`actions_goap_fusion_goal_budget_test.go`), which asserts the agent-scope store holds the research-goal stamp after `PrioritizeGoapGoals` runs, and `TestClearSuperpowersPlanState_WipesDurableChargeStamps` (`actions_goap_fusion_test.go`), which asserts all four durable keys are gone (from both ChainState and the agent-scope store) after `clearSuperpowersPlanState` runs.

---

## ADR-130: `IsKnownChainKind` Closes the ChainAction chain_type / Leaf-Children Validation Gap at Authoring Time (Q1 Correctness, Complete)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q1 Correctness — Close the ChainAction pre-execution validation gap so a mistyped chain_type or nested children fails at authoring time, not at LLM-call runtime." Two related authoring-time holes existed for `ChainAction` nodes. First, `parseChainConfig` reads `ChainConfig.ChainType` from the segment before `:` in a node's `Name` (§8.2), but neither validation entry point (`validateNode`/`ValidateTree`, `walkValidate`/`ValidateTreeFull`) checked it against the declared `ChainKind` constants — a mistyped or stale `chain_type` produced no signal until the node ticked and `buildChainActionFn`'s dispatch `switch` (`internal/engine/chains.go`) fell through to its `default` case, setting `bb.Outcome = "chain_failed"`. Second, `ChainAction` was missing from the shared `leafNodeTypes` set (§8.13) even though `buildNode`'s `case "ChainAction":` (`tree.go:322-325`) builds a childless `BuildChainAction` leaf and never looks at `node.Children` — a `ChainAction` with declared children silently dropped its subtree at build time, the same gap class §8.13 had already closed for `Action`/`Condition`/`AlwaysSucceed`.

**Decision:** **Milestone 1:** `IsKnownChainKind(kind string) bool` (`internal/engine/chains.go`), backed by a package-level `knownChainKinds` map covering all 11 declared `ChainKind` constants (`llm_call` through `tool_action`), reports whether a string matches one exactly. **Milestone 2:** `validateNode`'s new `case "ChainAction":` (`internal/engine/validate.go`) calls `parseChainConfig` and appends `<name>: unknown chain_type %q` to `ValidateTree`'s messages when `IsKnownChainKind` reports false. **Milestone 3:** `"ChainAction": true` was added to `leafNodeTypes` (`internal/engine/verifier.go`), so the pre-existing shared-map mechanism (§8.13) now also rejects a `ChainAction` node that declares `Children`, via both `walkValidate` (`ValidateTreeFull`) and `validateNode` (`ValidateTree`). **Milestone 4:** `walkValidate`'s type switch (`internal/engine/verifier.go`) gained its own `case "ChainAction":`, parsing the node's `ChainConfig` via `parseChainConfig` and appending the same `<name>: unknown chain_type %q` message to `ValidateTreeFull`'s `info.Errors` when `IsKnownChainKind` reports false — closing the gap milestone 2 left in the deep validator.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — all 4 milestones landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A mistyped or unsupported `chain_type` now fails `ValidateTree` at authoring time with the offending value named in the message, instead of only surfacing as a `chain_failed` outcome the first time the node ticks.
- ✅ `ChainAction` now shares §8.13's leaf-children invariant: a `ChainAction` declaring `Children` fails both `ValidateTreeFull` and `ValidateTree` instead of silently building into a tree that drops the subtree.
- ✅ The `chain_type` check is now independently enforced by both validation entry points: `validateNode`/`ValidateTree` (milestone 2) and `walkValidate`/`ValidateTreeFull` (milestone 4) each append their own `unknown chain_type %q` error, so a caller that validates exclusively through either path still catches a mistyped `chain_type` at authoring time.
- Pinned by `TestIsKnownChainKind` (`internal/engine/chains_test.go`), `TestValidateTree_ChainAction_UnknownChainType`, `TestValidateTree_ChainAction_KnownChainType`, `TestValidateTree_ChainAction_MixedTree` (`internal/engine/validate_test.go`), and `TestVerifierFull_ChainActionRejectsChildren`, `TestVerifierFull_ChainActionUnknownChainType`, `TestVerifierFull_ChainActionKnownChainType` (`internal/engine/verifier_test.go`).
